#2132. Peter Schmeichel fumes at Man United for 'very disrespectful' treatment of Michael Carrick as Mauricio Pochettino closes in on Old Trafford job - with club legend put out of his misery on Thursday

Published: 15:20 EST, 11 November 2026 | Updated: 06:48 EST, 12 November 2026

Peter Schmeichel has hit out at the 'very disrespectful' way that he believes beleaguered Manchester United manager Michael Carrick is being treated as he awaits his sacking at Old Trafford.

The former club captain has endured a torrid start to the season, and while the Man United hierarchy publicly backed him as recently as three weeks ago, Carrick is now set to be handed his marching orders following a crisis summit in Monaco.

Man United chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox flew to the Principality to meet with co-owner Sir Jim Ratcliffe to ratify the decision to move on from Carrick, with current USA head coach Mauricio Pochettino set to be appointed in his wake.

But with Carrick still taking team training at Carrington while plans for his ejection from the club made global headlines, Schmeichel admitted he felt deep sympathy for the former midfielder's awkward position.

'I think the way this is playing out is very disrespectful to him,' Schmeichel said on The Overlap Daily. 'If I’m honest, I don’t think he’s doing anything wrong.

Michael Carrick remains in limbo at Manchester United after a shocking start to the season

Omar Berrada and Jason Wilcox have been in Monaco discussing his dismissal this week

'I know the team that he’s in charge of is where they are in the table, but I don’t put all of that on him. There are so many things that have been going on at Manchester United literally since Alex (Ferguson) and (former chief executive) David Gill left the club. And it’s never been corrected.

'It’s not been the correct people, not the right people in charge, and slowly it’s just gone worse.'

Man United sit within the relegation zone as this international break plays out, with Carrick's men winless in the Premier League since late August.

Last-minute comebacks against Roma in the Champions League and Aston Villa in domestic action however may have briefly boosted morale at Old Trafford.

But to Schmeichel, the issue lies at the feet of those who recruit at the club, rather than Carrick himself.

'If you think about this transfer window now, it’s abundantly clear to everyone - everyone who knows just a little bit about football - that Manchester United needed defenders,' he continued. 'They got through a whole transfer window without signing a single defender.

'There was a little bit of talk about Lewis Hall joining the club or going for Lewis Hall. That didn’t happen. And so, they bought three midfield players. And we know, because we’ve been in the game for so many years, that you can’t win games - if you concede 30 goals in 10 games, then there is a problem, and that was not addressed.

'Now, was Michael Carrick in on those decisions? Well, it took them forever, forever, to give him the job. So, I suspect he wasn’t in any of those discussions about who to sign up until the point he got named as manager.

Former Man United goalkeeper Peter Schmeichel believes the club's treatment of Carrick has been demeaning

The under-fire manager turned up to take training for a second day in a row as speculation swirled over his future

'So yes, I do feel a lot of sympathy for Michael. I think what is happening is wrong. I think we should stick with Michael. I think we should just try and steady the ship a little bit and then get into January and then sign the players that he feels he needs to bring the team forward and then be realistic about what this season is all about. Obviously, it’s about climbing as high as Manchester United can in the table.'

By contrast, Schmeichel's co-pundit Tim Sherwood said he did not feel sorry for Carrick, because he will have understood the brutal situation he was stepping into at Man United.

The former Middlesbrough head coach served as interim manager for the second-half of the previous season following the sacking of Ruben Amorim, and was named on a permanent basis over the summer.

But while Schmeichel admitted that the life cycle of a Premier League coach was undeniably short, his issue is with the manner of treatment Carrick has received in the process of the changing of the guard in the dugout.

'If they’re looking for somebody else, then relieve the man of his duties,' the former goalkeeper argued. 'Don’t let him come into training yesterday. He took training yesterday. How disrespectful is that?

'How do you think this fella – I mean, he’s a human being. He’s a very, very good human being. He’s one of the nicest people you’ll ever meet in your life, and obviously you two know him well. So, treat the guy with respect. Tell him, "Sorry, mate. We don’t think that you are the future here. Here’s a cheque. Move on." But they’re not doing that. They’re dragging it out.'

Schmeichel was incensed too by Carrick's status within the club, wondering aloud whether, since they had taken 'weeks and weeks' to come to their messy, public decision, the club's hierarchy might not really understand what it is to truly represent the club.

'Do you think these people know? Do you think they care?' Schmeichel continued. 'These people are not Manchester United people.

'They don’t understand what it’s like to be one of the Manchester United people. They don’t know that. This is obviously part of the broader issue here, part of the reason the club is where they are now. I’m not saying I feel sorry for Michael, but I’m a little bit angry at how this great football club is treating one of its greatest players. Simple as that.'

Talks have been planned with Pochettino overnight on Wednesday, with the club's hierarchy thought to be preparing to dismiss Carrick face-to-face on Thursday.

Mauricio Pochettino remains locked in talks to take over at Old Trafford as the club's preferred candidate for the role

Pochettino is currently at the US Soccer base outside Atlanta preparing for a CONCACAF Nations League quarter-final double header against Haiti, and is due to speak to the media on Friday.

It’s unclear if he will remain in charge for those games. It is possible he could stay for the first leg in Kingston, Jamaica at the weekend and then travel to Manchester.

The second leg in Cincinnati is not until the early hours of Thursday, and it seems inconceivable that Pochettino would only take over at United on Friday with a huge game against Liverpool at Anfield 48 hours later.

It’s understood that the US Soccer Federation are ready for this contingency and have a succession plan in place should he leave.

It will cost Pochettino around £7million to buy himself out of his contract with the US Soccer Federation, with United ready to foot the bill.

The cost of bringing in his back-room team, including trusted assistants Jesus Perez and Miguel D’Agostino, will take the cost above £10m.

Meanwhile in Manchester, Carrick took training for the second day in a row at the club's Carrington training ground on Wednesday, arriving at 7.45am and departing shortly after 1.30pm.

Carrick has overseen the club's worst start to the season in 40 years, but the man himself remained adamant that he would not be falling on his sword after battling back to claim a draw against Villa.

In an awkward interview with Sky Sports' Patrick Davison, Carrick remained steadfast in the face of questions as to whether he would quit his role amid the turbulence.

'You know the situation, we haven't won enough games,' he said. 'But I expect to move forward with things we've been working on, showing a lot of the good from today tighten up a few mistakes and win some football matches.'

#2131. What Phil Neumann has said about his Birmingham City future as Nottingham Forest circle

Birmingham City defender Phil Neumann has broken his silence after a fresh round of transfer speculation recently came to the fore involving the German centre-back.

Neumann has been one of Blues' standout performers so far this season as Chris Davies' side sit top of the Championship, with the division returning to action this weekend after the final international break of 2026.

Birmingham's recent form of six wins in seven games whilst boasting the second tier's meanest defensive record further highlights a vast improvement on last season's 10th-placed finish, with Neumann one of many players in the Second City who struggled for overall consistency in 2025/26.

That didn't stop the 6"3 figure from becoming the subject of transfer interest in January and, indeed, the summer window, although it is apparent Neumann isn't giving recent exit talk much thought at this moment in time, which will appease the Blues fanbase and Davies in equal measure.

Neumann, 29, officially signed for Birmingham on last season's July 1st start date, having penned a pre-contract agreement during his final months with 2.Bundesliga outfit, Hannover 96.

In his first Championship season, the towering centre-back featured 32 times in a defence which changed personnel on several occasions, but has been an ever-present this term alongside captain, Christoph Klarer.

Neumann attracted interest from Sassuolo in January and then from Premier League newcomers, Hull City and Bundesliga side, Borussia Mönchengladbach in the summer, but remained at St. Andrew's to aid Blues' flying start to the season.

After also netting twice against Wrexham in August, the defender recently became the subject of interest from Nottingham Forest and Fiorentina, before further links emerged over potential moves to Hull, Coventry City and Ipswich Town as early as January.

The Recklinghausen-born man has never featured in a top European division in his career so far, making 190 appearances in 2. Bundesliga for the likes of Hannover and Holstein Kiel, as well as his 47 Championship outings.

Speaking to Transfermarkt, Neumann admits that the Championship's physical nature has certainly brought the best out of him, whilst also reaffirming his commitment towards the cause of helping Blues realise their promotion ambitions despite the strong exit talk.

"Of course I hear about those kinds of reports, but I don't pay much attention to them. I have my advisors for that, people I trust who handle those matters," he said.

"My full focus is on Birmingham City and what we've set out to achieve as a team. There's nothing more important to me than doing everything I can to achieve our goals with the club.

"We have a big task ahead of us, and that's where I'm concentrating all my energy. Everything else is secondary at the moment," Neumann stated.

Blues fans, are you reassured about Phil Neumann's immediate future given these comments? Comment below!

"I'm in a good sporting position. Therefore, if I were to consider another option, the whole package would have to be right.

"The league is a factor, but by no means the only one. I want to know what role I'm expected to play, how the coach wants us to play, and what the club's future holds."

The way in which Davies has altered Birmingham's style this season has clearly suited Neumann down to the ground, as well as the fact he has played all 1,350 Championship minutes.

It has been debated whether he would receive such numbers at the City Ground, despite the fact Forest could benefit from extra defensive depth due to Oliver Glasner's three-back system.

Liam Millar's immediate future has been debated with his Hull City contract expiring next June

It is also clear that, at this stage of his career, Neumann is one of several players who are excited about what the future holds for Birmingham, including the planned move to the Powerhouse Stadium.

Whether he sees out the next five years in the West Midlands remains to be seen, but the chance of becoming a cult figure by helping return the club to the top-flight is unsurprisingly a major factor in his thought process.

The Argentinian defender has also apologized to the United higher ups as well over his comments, as shared by Fabrizio Romano.

Lisandro Martinez has held discussions with Jason Wilcox and Michael Carrick after speaking out following United’s 2-0 defeat to Atletico Madrid.

Martinez had questioned whether Carrick was backed enough in the summer and suggested the club needed changes.

Fabrizio Romano has now confirmed that United consider the matter resolved for now and expect Martinez to remain available for selection.

Romano also revealed that United had imposed a financial penalty on the centre-back. However, the punishment does not appear to involve removing him from the squad.

“According to the information available tonight, Lisandro Martinez has been fined.”

He added that Martinez is expected to return to the squad as normal, with the fine considered sufficient punishment for the incident.

“The internal discussion also helped to resolve all the issues. For Man Utd, the case is closed.”

Romano also pointed towards Martinez’s contract, which expires next summer, with United holding an option to extend it.

“So, let’s see what is going to happen there. For sure, this is the kind of situation that will have an impact on the situation surrounding Lisandro, but we have to see what happens next in terms of future transfers and everything else.”

#2129. Cardinale says MSE will give Milan ‘financial muscle’ and reiterates: “I will never sell”

Gerry Cardinale has commented on the launch of AC Milan’s new holding company, Milan Sports & Entertainment (MSE), stating that he will never sell the club.

Yesterday, it was confirmed that RedBird had launched the new holding company to ‘consolidate RedBird’s Milan-based investments in sports, infrastructure, and entertainment’. From the new stadium to the NBA Europa project, there are big plans for the company.

Brett Yormark has been appointed as the CEO of MSE after impressing as the Commissioner of the Big 12 Conference (college sports), labelling it ‘an honour’ to get the position. It doesn’t mean that we will see less of Cardinale though, quite the opposite.

In an interview with Gazzetta dello Sport this morning, Cardinale outlined exactly what he wants to achieve with MSE and explained why he will never sell the club. Silvio Berlusconi 2.0, as he calls it, with the goal of taking Milan back to the very top.

Cardinale, what benefits will AC Milan gain from the holding company?

“The driving force behind deals like this is to provide AC Milan with the financial muscle needed to compete in the transfer market and secure the best players in the world.

“I will transform the club’s financial profile with the resources that will come from the stadium, the San Donato Arena, and the other activities we are working on with NBA Europe.

“I’m doing everything I can to ensure that, when we want a player, no one can tell me, ‘You can’t sign him because you can’t afford him.’ Currently, only Premier League clubs are able to invest such sums and secure top players.

“If I want to bring AC Milan back to the top of European competition, not just in Italy, we need to figure out how to compete with the world’s top clubs.”

This project confirms that your commitment to Milan is long-term…

“Yes. I will never sell AC Milan. Never. And this is a project to get them back to winning ways. I’m not willing to tolerate it taking a long time to achieve this. I want to do it as soon as possible. We’ll see how quickly…

“My goal is to consistently win Scudetti and, of course, return to the Champions League. Milan Sports & Entertainment represents the foundation to make all this possible.”

The connection with Milan deserves a separate mention: RedBird and the city are now even more connected…

“This is the legacy we will leave in Milan. The stadium, obviously, represents a huge legacy that will transform our financial profile, but then there’s also the San Donato development project and the rest.

“If we want AC Milan to become great again, we need to close the revenue gap with the Premier League clubs. When I bought the club four years ago, I used the expression ‘Berlusconi 2.0’ and no one understood what I meant. Now perhaps we are starting to see it.”

“Everyone thought I was making an arrogant, self-centred comparison between me and Berlusconi, but that wasn’t my point. What I was trying to explain was that, just as Berlusconi transformed AC Milan and Serie A, we want to be able, through our ownership, to lead the club into a new era.

“We need to do it in the same way Berlusconi did, albeit in a modern way. I’m used to working a certain way because that’s how I do things, at the highest levels worldwide and in America.”

Could your “recipe” also be valid for Serie A?

“Of course, even for Serie A. In my opinion, it wouldn’t have been a good deal to sell 30% of the league’s international TV rights when JP Morgan made that offer. That’s not how Serie A can return to the top of Europe.

“It needs to be done through business planning and by creating every opportunity to generate new revenue. Like we will do with Milan Sports & Entertainment.

“We will be the first to build a stadium since 2011, and that’s a huge achievement. I started working on it when I bought AC Milan, and finally… the right time has arrived.”

Do you think others will follow you on this path?

“No. Some may do it, others may not. We know what it takes to innovate and change. To avoid continuing to practice Einstein’s definition of insanity, according to which everyone wants a different outcome, but doesn’t want to do anything different to get it.”

How long do you think it will take to complete the other projects that will be part of the new holding company?

“I think the NBA is negotiating with the Euroleague, but one way or another, if they can’t reach an agreement, NBA Europe will still launch. Perhaps the first batch of new franchises for Europe will be announced at the end of the year or in early 2027.

“We will see when that happens… We own the land in San Donato, and if I’m granted the Milan franchise, I will be able to start construction fairly quickly because we already have planning permission for a 20,000-seat arena.

“We will also continue to explore opportunities and hold discussions within the tennis world and to organise a tournament in Milan. Finally, we are already making progress in building and launching a hospitality and facility management company.”

Does your plan include organising and managing hospitality services for other clubs’ stadiums as well, not just the new San Siro?

“Yes, the model is the company I created in 2009, called Legends Hospitality. I created it with the New York Yankees and the Dallas Cowboys. Now that company is worth several billion dollars.

“We not only sell premium seats, suites, merchandise, and hospitality, food, and beverage services for Yankee Stadium and Cowboys Stadium, but also for other clubs. I would like to adopt the same model here, probably in partnership with another club.”

Not many football clubs in the world have established an organisation like the one you are leading…

“Exactly. My ambition is to compete with the best clubs in the world. I want to do so thanks to my global vision of the world of sports and entertainment.

“There aren’t many multi-sports platforms in the world that also own some of their own facilities, and we want to add AC Milan to that list. To compete with them and then be better.”

In an effort to generate new revenue, are you also considering a tour for AC Milan next summer? Will that still be Australia or the United States?

“We have already discussed it with Amorim, and I will just tell you that if we play friendlies abroad, they will be in the United States next summer. If we do… We haven’t decided yet, and the decision will be up to Ruben.

“It will depend on what’s best for the team: playing friendlies abroad must add value to the group, not bring in a couple of million for the club. Those extra two million will no longer be our goal, as they were under the previous management. That won’t happen again under this ownership.

“Everything we organise in terms of friendlies will be carefully considered with our coach. Every move. And we won’t participate in tours organised by others. We put our players first. We want to help them prepare well to win. That’s our goal, and we aim to achieve it as soon as possible.”

Gerry is right. You are wrong. Look it up.

The English Premiere League makes about FOUR TIMES as much money in TV broadcast rights as Serie A does – €4.6 billion per season (£3.84 billion – £1.68 billion domestic, £2.17 billion international).

Serie A’s TV broadcast rights give it €1.144 billion per season (€894 million domestic, €250 million international)

Total stadium revenue among all the clubs in the English Premier League is also about 2.3 x higher than total stadium revenue for all the clubs in Serie A – over £1billion per season in the EPL (€1.3 billion) compared with €435.4 million per season in Serie A.

It was not always this way. In the 1980s, English football was beset with old, rotting, unsafe stadiums, hooliganism, and low TV revenues.

In 1992, the First Division clubs broke away from the rest of the English Football League (EFL) to form the Premiere League, and began negotiating TV contracts on their own, as well as embarking on a program of enlarging and upgrading their football stadiums. The richest teams began building completely new, large modern stadiums with premium suites, etc.

Meanwhile, nothing much has changed in Italy – most of the stadiums remain municipal owned and small and old.

Ticket prices in the EPL are also generally on average about twice as expensive as in Italy.

So yeah, Serie A had its glory days in the 1990s, as wealthy magnates like Berlusconi infused their personal wealth into their clubs. I remember Fox carried Serie A football on TV in the United States back then. If you wanted to watch the English Premiere League, it was a bit of a struggle to find the matches on TV in the U.S back in the 1990s.

All that changed as the Premiere League steadily built up its international marketing and negotiated ever improved TV contracts. It began to require clubs that were promoted up to the EPL to upgrade their stadiums to meet a certain minimum seating capacity.

And so, today, when Italians complain about how Italy and Serie A can no longer compete with the English Premiere League for the best players, how the EPL hugely overpays for all its star players, the reason is that the EPL makes a $HIT TON MORE MONEY than Serie A does, across the board, in TV income (the biggest factor for the entire league), and stadium income.

That’s why Gerry wanted a new and better stadium, and why he has often talked about Serie A needing to do better marketing and improve its TV contracts.

You can’t get the best players in the world if you can’t compete financially with the richest and most watched football league in the world, which happens to be the English Premiere League right now.

Here in the US, EPL matches are carried by NBC Sports by subscription (about $130-$200 a year). NBC pays the Premiere League $450 million per season to carry English football on TV in the USA.

Serie A matches are carried by Paramount/CBS in the USA (a $90 per year subscription – it went up from $60 after the Ellison Oligarchy mergers). CBS had been paying Serie A $75.8 million per year for those TV rights. However, Serie A with its great marketing and negotiating expertise was unable to improve or renew that contract and so Serie A is currently just going year to year with CBS and getting less than $30 million a year.

Do you see the difference now in the major sources of income between the Premiere League and Serie A??

So yeah, absolutely, Gerry is right to complain about needing to get Serie A some better negotiators and marketers to improve on its TV contracts.

#2128. Will Lisandro Martinez play against Leeds after explosive rant?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Lisandro Martinez could return to Manchester United’s starting line-up against Leeds United this weekend, with the defender reportedly available for selection after the controversy surrounding his comments about the club’s summer transfer business.

TEAMtalk transfer insider Graeme Bailey has suggested Michael Carrick could bring the Argentine straight back into the team for the trip to Elland Road. The fixture is expected to be a hostile occasion, making Martinez’s potential involvement an interesting one.

“I am told that Martinez is available for selection this weekend, and there is every chance Michael Carrick starts him,” Bailey said.

The defender recently found himself in trouble after questioning whether Manchester United had done enough in the summer transfer window. He apologised to the club’s hierarchy and was fined, but the incident has not apparently created a divide between him and the manager.

Bailey also dismissed suggestions that Martinez’s comments were an attack on Michael Carrick, insisting the defender and his teammates continue to support the head coach.

“Martinez was not critical of Carrick – far from it. This isn’t a repeat of something like Mo Salah criticising Arne Slot last season. It is almost the opposite.”

According to Bailey, the United dressing room remains firmly behind Carrick despite the team’s disappointing start to the campaign. Senior players reportedly believe the current manager is still the right person to lead the team, rather than bringing in another face to replace him.

“Martinez and the squad are 100% behind Carrick. They know there is pressure after a poor start to the season, but they do not want to see a change.”

Bailey went on to suggest that the fallout may have strengthened the bond between the players and their manager.

“Martinez and the senior players firmly believe nobody can come in and do a better job than Carrick. If anything, this has brought the group closer together.”

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

#2127. Neil Warnock reacts to James Bond announcement in hilarious way

Former Queens Park Rangers and Sheffield United manager Neil Warnock has reacted to Robert Pattinson being cast as the new James Bond in a hilarious social media post.

Neil Warnock and Steve Bruce have the most promotions from the second tier to the top flight, while Warnock has eight promotions across all divisions. That places the 77-year-old with the outright record in English football history.

However, he has not managed in the EFL since Huddersfield Town in 2023, with short stints at Aberdeen and Torquay United recently. He instead was eyeing a different role away from football following a tongue-in-cheek social media post.

Daniel Craig played James Bond for 15 years between his first film appearance in 2006 and his final film in 2021, starring in five movies. However, for years now, there has been speculation over who would replace him.

That has been filled by Robert Pattinson, but former Queens Park Rangers and Sheffield United boss Warnock has reacted to the decision. Firstly, he wrote: "You’ve got to live and let die for 3 points."

Another response was to promote his new range of socks, with Warnock also stating: "The names Warnock…Neil Warnock: Chief of Socks 🧦"

The 25 top scorers in the Championship since the second tier was rebranded back in 2004/05, including Billy Sharp and Aleksandar Mitrovic.

However, the most hilarious response from Warnock included an edited version of Pattinson's announcement post, which also said: "I told them at the audition that I was only available until the end of the season. Never heard back 🤷‍♂️"

Much like 007, Warnock spent a career taking on impossible missions and invariably walking away with the job done. Armed not with a Walther PPK, but a sharp tongue and a well-worn clipboard, English football’s ultimate troubleshooter carved out a record-breaking legacy across four decades in the dugout.

With a staggering eight career promotions, Warnock repeatedly turned relegation-threatened strugglers into overachievers. From Notts County and Sheffield United, to QPR and Cardiff City, he possessed a unique licence to thrill, baffling rivals, and delighting supporters with his unmistakable brand of grit and charisma.

What's your favourite Neil Warnock moment? Comment your thoughts below!

And just like Bond, whenever you thought you had seen the last of him, Warnock was always guaranteed to return. He regularly stepped back out of retirement for one final mission whenever a club needed saving. The similarities are clear, aren't they?

Warnock's post led to some fantastic responses in the replies on X (formerly Twitter), with one stating: "The name's Colin..."

Another reaction on X read: "Live and Let Die for Three Points"

A further reply to Warnock added: "From Route One With Love"

One fan thought Warnock was perfect for the Bond role, adding: "This would have been the greatest casting in film history, we've been robbed"
